### F82. FixedEndSpline at lam>0 has corners at knots 1 and n-2

✅ proven · ⚪ low · [steering/manifold_paths.py:182](../../steering/manifold_paths.py#L182)

- **Claim:** _fit_fixed_ends pins gamma_int[0] and gamma_int[m-1] to zero and drops their equations. This also drops the C1 (slope-continuity) condition at the first and last interior knots, so for any lam>0 the curve has a corner there. bending_energy() takes those gammas as 0 and does not count the corners, so it under-reports. The curve also does not converge to the lam=0 interpolant as lam->0. The README and the --persona-lam help say the curve stays C2-smooth at any lam.
- **Failure case:** An 8-knot curve in 5-D (y scale 3) at lam=1e-3: the one-sided derivatives at knot 1 differ by 574, about equal to |S'| itself, so the curve has a corner. At lam=1e-9 the knots move by only 2e-4, but the curve differs from the lam=0 curve by up to 10.0 units. Bending energy falls 29x (2.3e6 vs 6.6e7) and arc length falls from 118.5 to 91.7. So `geometry_check --persona-lam <any >0>` and smoke's lam=1.0 path report detour, overshoot and bending_energy for a kinked curve, and these numbers cannot be compared with the lam=0 rows.
- **Test result:** The finding is true. Real FixedEndSpline.evaluate shows a slope jump about 1-2x |S'| at knots 1 and n-2 for lam>0, while the lam=0 control and the middle knots stay C1 (rel <1e-5). As lam->0, the knots converge (move 4e-7) but the curve stays 5.7 units from the interpolant, and the same corner appears through PersonaPath. This matches the code: gamma_int[0] and gamma_int[m-1] are set to zero and their equations are dropped (manifold_paths.py:182-186). The exact numbers in the finding text differ from the rerun and need correction.
- **Test output:** 8 knots, 5-D, y scale 3, seed 0. - lam=0 control: relative slope jump is at most 7e-6 at every knot. - lam=1e-3: knot 1 jump is 163.2 (|S'| about 95.6, rel 1.71). Knot 6 (n-2) jump is 42.1 (|S'| about 35.1, rel 1.20). Knots 2-5 have rel about 1e-6. gamma[1] = gamma[n-2] = 0. - lam=1e-9: knots move at most 4.3e-4, but max |S_lam - S_0| = 5.70. ### F87. Role 'assistant' collides with non-role verdict 'assistant'

◐ proven, narrower · ⚪ low · [steering/persona_judge.py:59](../../steering/persona_judge.py#L59)

- **Claim:** load_candidates returns every role stem except 'default', and the role list includes a role literally named 'assistant' (refs/assistant-axis/roles/instructions/assistant.json). The schema enum is `candidates + NON_ROLE`, so 'assistant' appears twice. The verdict 'assistant' cannot tell 'speaks as the assistant role' apart from 'identifies as an AI' (the outcome that 'wins even if the response also mentions a role'). Duplicate enum values may also be rejected by the structured-output validator, which would fail every call.
- **Failure case:** In cells for role 'assistant' (likely one of the roles nearest the Assistant end), any AI-identifying response reads as on-target persona adoption. Elsewhere, a response that adopts the 'assistant' role is counted as reverting to the AI Assistant. Trajectories through persona space then merge two different outcomes. If the API rejects the non-unique enum, every row comes back persona=None, ERROR_BadRequestError.
- **Test result:** The finding is true for its main claim. The real load_candidates() returns 'assistant' from refs/assistant-axis/roles/instructions/assistant.json, which is a normal role prompt ("You are an assistant dedicated to..."). The real _params() then makes an enum of 278 values with only 277 unique, because 'assistant' appears twice. The prompt also lists 'assistant' as a candidate role, and the system rule keeps the same label for "identifies as an AI". So the judge's output cannot tell the two outcomes apart. The test does not check whether the API rejects the duplicate enum.
- **Test output:** n candidates: 275; 'assistant' in load_candidates(): True; NON_ROLE: ['assistant','unclear','nonsensical']; enum length 278, unique 277; duplicate enum values: {'assistant': 2} (expected none); 'assistant' listed in candidate line: True; system prompt reserves 'assistant' for AI: True; AI-identity row persona=assistant and role-adoption row persona=assistant (identical: True).
- **Test file:** [repro/F87_test.py](../../output/code_review_2026-10-08/repro/F87_test.py) · my re-run: BUG_REPRODUCED

### F88. Validation sample includes unjudged rows; kappa crashes after labelling

✅ proven · ⚪ low · [steering/validate_judge.py:148](../../steering/validate_judge.py#L148)

Those rows go to the key file as empty values. compute_kappa then reads them as NaN, `astype(str)` turns them into 'nan', and the bad_j check raises. The real compute_kappa then raised "labels outside CATEGORIES - human [], judge [nan]". A control frame with no None scores passed. The real parse_verdict returns judge_score=None for a bad reply, and judge.py keeps those rows in judged_L19.parquet. So real judged frames can contain unjudged rows.
- **Test output:** parse_verdict on bad JSON -> None; judged frame has 200 rows with 10 None; key has 100 rows with 3 empty judge_score (expected 0); compute_kappa raised ValueError "labels outside CATEGORIES - human [], judge [nan]"; the control with no None rows gives n_labelled=100 and no error.

### F101. G5 'sourced' check ignores sign, so flipped signs pass

✅ proven · ⚪ low · [.claude/skills/presenting-results/gate_check.py:74](../../.claude/skills/presenting-results/gate_check.py#L74)

- **Claim:** report_number_key takes abs() of every report number, and rounded_forms adds abs() forms of every source number. So G5 cannot tell r = +0.66 from r = -0.66. The gate also accepts a match at 1, 2 or 3 decimals against every float in the source files. For large CSV sources, almost any 2-decimal number matches.
- **Failure case:** A report says 'partial r = 0.66' or '+0.66' and the source ladder CSV has -0.656214. report_number_key gives 0.66, rounded_forms(-0.656214) contains round(abs(x),2)=0.66, and G5 prints PASS. The gate exists to catch unsourced or mis-copied numbers, but it misses a sign error.
- **Test result:** The finding is true. I re-ran the test. The real gate_check gives '+0.66', '0.66' and '-0.66' the same key, and it puts both signed and abs() forms of each source float into the pool. So G5 PASSes when the report sign is the opposite of the source sign. The control '0.71' gives WARN as expected. The NUM regex keeps the sign, so the sign is lost in report_number_key (line 74/76) and rounded_forms (lines 51/53).
- **Test output:** report_number_key('0.66')=0.66, '+0.66'=0.66, '-0.66'=0.66; 0.66 in rounded_forms(-0.656214)=True; G5 '+0.66' vs -0.656214 -> PASS; '-0.66' vs 0.656214 -> PASS; '0.66' vs -0.656214 -> PASS; control '0.71' -> WARN; big CSV: 198/198 two-decimal values match, 797/999 three-decimal values match.
- **Test file:** [repro/F101_test.py](../../output/code_review_2026-10-08/repro/F101_test.py) · my re-run: BUG_REPRODUCED

### F102. Empirical chance counts self-matches as misses, biasing chance down

◐ proven, narrower · ⚪ low · [experiments/q1_emergence/261005_base_incontext_elicitation/score.py:84](../../experiments/q1_emergence/261005_base_incontext_elicitation/score.py#L84)

- **Claim:** The shuffled-role null scores each reply against a permuted role. When the permutation gives back the reply's own role, the code counts the pair as a miss (`pr != r['role']`) instead of drawing again or dropping it. The chance rate is then too low, which makes the pass rule `b['lo'] > b['chance']` easier to meet.
- **Failure case:** Each role has 2 replies per (stage, cond), so about 2 of 120 permuted pairs get their own role. Those pairs always score 0, so chance drops by about 1.7% of its value. The effect is small, but its direction always favors the pass verdict for rule 1/2 in results.json.
- **Test result:** The finding is true. At line 84, summarize counts a permuted pair that gets its own role back as a miss and keeps it in the denominator. On a case where the true wrong-role chance is 1.0, the real code returns 0.983, so the chance estimate is about 1.7% too low. The effect is real but small. On the stored data, no rule-2 verdict changes: lo is 0.10–0.22 and chance is about 0.02.
- **Test output:** Test 1: every wrong role is in top, so the expected chance is 1.0000. summarize() gives 0.9830, a shortfall of 0.0170. The self-match rate per pair is about 0.0167. Test 2: top holds 5 random wrong roles. The exact wrong-role chance with self-matches excluded is 0.08475. The exact expectation with self-matches scored as 0 is 0.08333. Over 20 calls, summarize() gives 0.08325 (se 0.00040). The relative bias is -1.67%. Real data: lo is 0.10 to 0.22 and chance is about 0.018 to 0.022. A corrected chance moves up by only about 0.0003, so no rule-2 outcome changes.
- **Test file:** [repro/F102_test.py](../../output/code_review_2026-10-08/repro/F102_test.py) · my re-run: BUG_REPRODUCED

## Refuted in the first review (not tested again)

- `src/manifold_persona/runlog.py:101` holm() is order-dependent and returns 1.0 for all tests when one p is NaN. The holm() behaviour itself is real. With a NaN input the sort order is undefined, and max/min then turn the result into 1.0 for every entry or for only some entries, depending on insertion order. I reproduced this exactly. But the failure scenario the …
- `exploratory/per_persona/study_qa_sweep.py:86` Anchor-subset check is vacuous; question identity never verified. The finding's main failure scenario does not hold, for three reasons. 1. question_idx is a stable global index. It is not a per-dataset ordering. src/manifold_persona/prompts_roles.py:build_role_records samples indices with range(len(questions)) over the …
- `steering/smoke.py:390` Hook-layer check uses a relative match tolerance but an absolute neighbour test. The finding's scenario assumes that a hook one layer off still gives n_lo ≈ 4. That does not happen. The hook is a forward hook on the output of model.layers[idx] (activation_steering.py:463), and the hook returns activations + 0. HF hidden_states[i] for i >= …
- `robustness/current/calibrate_tolerances.py:180` Tolerances from bootstrap SD and absolute floors make some pins vacuous. The arithmetic in the finding is correct. invariants.json pins prompt.interaction_frac at 0.005879 with tol 0.005, and the bootstrap SD is 0.00012. On its own row, that tolerance is 85% of the value. But the failure scenario says a drift to 0.0033 would pass …
